You are the second reviewer on a routine security code review of our own
nix-bitcoin fork: the NixOS modules and packaging we deploy on our own Bitcoin
and Lightning node. Two prior reviewers have already produced the findings
below against the exact commit checked out in the current directory. Your job
is quality control on their work, as the maintainer's independent check:

For EACH finding, open the cited file at the cited location and decide:
- verdict: "confirmed" (the defect is real as described), "false-positive"
  (the code does not do what the finding claims, or an existing control
  already prevents it — name the control), or "needs-info" (cannot be decided
  from the tree; say what is missing).
- severity: your own call (critical/high/medium/low/info), which may differ
  from the reviewer's.
- reasoning: one to three sentences grounded in the code you read (quote the
  line or construct that decides it).
- fix_ok: whether the reviewer's recommended fix is correct and complete
  (true/false) and, if false, what is wrong with it.

Then, only for the files the findings touch, note up to three defects the
reviewers missed or misread that a maintainer should know about ("missed").
Do not pad: if there is nothing, say so.

Read-only tools only; do not modify the tree. Be terse and concrete.

End your answer with exactly one fenced ```json block of this shape:
{"reviews":[{"id":"<finding id or title>","verdict":"confirmed|false-positive|needs-info","severity":"...","reasoning":"...","fix_ok":true,"fix_note":""}],
 "missed":[{"file":"...","line":0,"severity":"...","title":"...","description":"..."}],
 "summary":"two or three sentences"}
followed by nothing else.

     1	{ config, pkgs, lib, ... }:
     2	
     3	# Create a WireGuard server with a single peer.
     4	# Private/public keys are created via the secrets system.
     5	# Add helper binaries `nix-bitcoin-wg-connect` and optionally `lndconnect-wg`.
     6	
     7	# See ../../docs/services.md ("Use Zeus (mobile lightning wallet) via WireGuard")
     8	# for usage instructions.
     9	
    10	# This is a rather opinionated implementation that lacks the flexibility offered by
    11	# other nix-bitcoin modules, so ship this as a `preset`.
    12	# Some users will prefer to use `lndconnect` with their existing WireGuard or Tailscale setup.
    13	
    14	with lib;
    15	let
    16	  options.nix-bitcoin.wireguard = {
    17	    subnet = mkOption {
    18	      type = types.str;
    19	      default = "10.10.0";
    20	      description = "The /24 subnet of the wireguard network.";
    21	    };
    22	    restrictPeer = mkOption {
    23	      type = types.bool;
    24	      default = true;
    25	      description = ''
    26	        Prevent the peer from connecting to any addresses except for the WireGuard server address.
    27	      '';
    28	    };
    29	  };
    30	
    31	  cfg = config.nix-bitcoin.wireguard;
    32	  wgSubnet = cfg.subnet;
    33	  inherit (config.networking.wireguard.interfaces) wg-nb;
    34	  inherit (config.services) lnd;
    35	
    36	  lndconnect = lnd.enable && lnd.lndconnect.enable;
    37	
    38	  serverAddress = "${wgSubnet}.1";
    39	  peerAddress = "${wgSubnet}.2";
    40	
    41	  secretsDir = config.nix-bitcoin.secretsDir;
    42	
    43	  wgConnectUser = if config.nix-bitcoin.operator.enable
    44	                  then config.nix-bitcoin.operator.name
    45	                  else "root";
    46	
    47	  # A script that prints a QR code to connect a peer to the server.
    48	  # The QR code encodes a wg-quick config that can be imported by the wireguard
    49	  # mobile app.
    50	  wgConnect = pkgs.writers.writeBashBin "nix-bitcoin-wg-connect" ''
    51	    set -euo pipefail
    52	    text=
    53	    host=
    54	    for arg in "$@"; do
    55	      case $arg in
    56	        --text)
    57	          text=1
    58	          ;;
    59	        *)
    60	          host=$arg
    61	          ;;
    62	      esac
    63	    done
    64	
    65	    if [[ ! $host ]]; then
    66	      # Use lndconnect to fetch the external ip.
    67	      # This internally uses https://github.com/GlenDC/go-external-ip, which
    68	      # queries a set of external ip providers.
    69	      host=$(
    70	        ${getExe config.nix-bitcoin.pkgs.lndconnect} --url --nocert \
    71	          --configfile=/dev/null --adminmacaroonpath=/dev/null \
    72	          | sed -nE 's|.*?/(.*?):.*|\1|p'
    73	      )
    74	    fi
    75	
    76	    config="[Interface]
    77	    PrivateKey = $(cat ${secretsDir}/wg-peer-private-key)
    78	    Address = ${peerAddress}/24
    79	
    80	    [Peer]
    81	    PublicKey = $(cat ${secretsDir}/wg-server-public-key)
    82	    AllowedIPs = ${wgSubnet}.0/24
    83	    Endpoint = $host:${toString wg-nb.listenPort}
    84	    PersistentKeepalive = 25
    85	    "
    86	
    87	    if [[ $text ]]; then
    88	      echo "$config"
    89	    else
    90	      echo "$config" | ${getExe pkgs.qrencode} -t UTF8 -o -
    91	    fi
    92	  '';
    93	in {
    94	  inherit options;
    95	
    96	  config = {
    97	    assertions = [
    98	      {
    99	        # Don't support `netns-isolation` for now to keep things simple
   100	        assertion = !(config.nix-bitcoin.netns-isolation.enable or false);
   101	        message = "`nix-bitcoin.wireguard` is not compatible with `netns-isolation`.";
   102	      }
   103	    ];
   104	
   105	    networking.wireguard.interfaces.wg-nb = {
   106	      ips = [ "${serverAddress}/24" ];
   107	      listenPort = mkDefault 51820;
   108	      privateKeyFile = "${secretsDir}/wg-server-private-key";
   109	      allowedIPsAsRoutes = false;
   110	      peers = [
   111	        {
   112	          # To use the actual public key from the secrets file, use dummy pubkey
   113	          # `peer0` and replace it via `getPubkeyFromFile` (see further below)
   114	          # at peer service runtime.
   115	          publicKey = "peer0";
   116	          allowedIPs = [ "${peerAddress}/32" ];
   117	        }
   118	      ];
   119	    };
   120	
   121	    systemd.services = {
   122	      wireguard-wg-nb = rec {
   123	        wants = [ "nix-bitcoin-secrets.target" ];
   124	        after = wants;
   125	      };
   126	
   127	      # Read the peer pubkey from the secrets file at service runtime.
   128	      # (Upstream patched its own source with sed and re-executed it as root,
   129	      # which turned a secrets-store write into root RCE; audit L-8.)
   130	      wireguard-wg-nb-peer-peer0 = {
   131	        script = mkForce ''
   132	          ${pkgs.wireguard-tools}/bin/wg set wg-nb peer "$(cat "${secretsDir}/wg-peer-public-key")" allowed-ips ${peerAddress}/32
   133	        '';
   134	        postStop = mkForce ''
   135	          ${pkgs.wireguard-tools}/bin/wg set wg-nb peer "$(cat "${secretsDir}/wg-peer-public-key")" remove || true
   136	        '';
   137	      };
   138	    };
   139	
   140	    environment.systemPackages = [
   141	      wgConnect
   142	    ] ++ (optional lndconnect
   143	      (pkgs.writers.writeBashBin "lndconnect-wg" ''
   144	        exec lndconnect --host "${serverAddress}" --nocert "$@"
   145	      '')
   146	    );
   147	
   148	    networking.firewall = let
   149	      restrictPeerRule = "-s ${peerAddress} ! -d ${serverAddress} -j REJECT";
   150	    in {
   151	      allowedUDPPorts = [ wg-nb.listenPort ];
   152	
   153	      extraCommands =
   154	        optionalString lndconnect ''
   155	          # Admit lnd REST only from the single WireGuard peer, and only when the
   156	          # packet actually arrived over the wg-nb interface. `-s` alone matches
   157	          # a spoofed source on any interface; `-i` pins it to the tunnel (audit
   158	          # 2026-08-29, low). This is the interim hardening while the
   159	          # 0.0.0.0 bind itself stays deferred (see docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md).
   160	          iptables -w -A nixos-fw -i wg-nb -p tcp -s ${peerAddress}/32 --dport ${toString lnd.restPort} -j nixos-fw-accept
   161	        ''
   162	        + optionalString cfg.restrictPeer ''
   163	          # Insert at the top: appended (-A) the REJECT would sit behind the
   164	          # standard accept rules (allowed ports, the REST accept above), so a
   165	          # peer could still reach any firewall-allowed port on the node's
   166	          # other addresses through the tunnel (second opinion 2026-09-09).
   167	          # Replies to server-initiated traffic are unaffected: their
   168	          # destination is serverAddress.
   169	          iptables -w -I nixos-fw 1 ${restrictPeerRule}
   170	          iptables -w -I FORWARD 1 ${restrictPeerRule}
   171	        '';
   172	
   173	      extraStopCommands =
   174	        # Rules added to chain `nixos-fw` are automatically removed when restarting
   175	        # the NixOS firewall service.
   176	        mkIf cfg.restrictPeer ''
   177	          iptables -w -D FORWARD ${restrictPeerRule} || :
   178	        '';
   179	    };
   180	
   181	    # Listen on all addresses, including `serverAddress`.
   182	    # The listen port is guarded by the firewall (nixos-fw default-drops; only
   183	    # the wg subnet accept rule above admits REST traffic), but this is still
   184	    # a known audit finding (2026-08-24, advisory): the admin REST API relies
   185	    # on the firewall alone. Binding to `serverAddress` instead is DEFERRED —
   186	    # `nbLib.address` maps 0.0.0.0 to 127.0.0.1, so every internal consumer
   187	    # of `restAddress` (macaroon ExecStartPost, btcpayserver, lndconnect
   188	    # onion target) silently depends on this value, and a naive rebind breaks
   189	    # them plus lnd startup ordering. Read docs/wireguard-rest-bind.md and
   190	    # work through its checklist before changing this line.
   191	    services.lnd = mkIf lndconnect {
   192	      restAddress = "0.0.0.0";
   193	      tor.enforce = false;
   194	    };
   195	
   196	    nix-bitcoin.secrets = {
   197	      wg-server-private-key = {};
   198	      wg-server-public-key = { user = wgConnectUser; group = "root"; };
   199	      wg-peer-private-key = { user = wgConnectUser; group = "root"; };
   200	      wg-peer-public-key = {};
   201	    };
   202	
   203	    nix-bitcoin.generateSecretsCmds.wireguard = let
   204	      wg = "${pkgs.wireguard-tools}/bin/wg";
   205	    in ''
   206	      makeWireguardKey() {
   207	        local name=$1
   208	        local priv=wg-$name-private-key
   209	        local pub=wg-$name-public-key
   210	        if [[ ! -e $priv ]]; then
   211	          ${wg} genkey > $priv
   212	        fi
   213	        if [[ $priv -nt $pub ]]; then
   214	          ${wg} pubkey < $priv > $pub
   215	        fi
   216	      }
   217	      makeWireguardKey server
   218	      makeWireguardKey peer
   219	    '';
   220	  };
   221	}
     1	# RPC calls that are safe for public use.
     2	#
     3	# Safety bar: bounded CPU cost per call, no P2P side effects beyond
     4	# transaction relay, no leaks of peer topology or wallet contents.
     5	#
     6	# Deliberately excluded (audit M-3):
     7	# - scantxoutset, gettxoutsetinfo, getblocktemplate: minutes-scale CPU,
     8	#   trivially abused for DoS by any holder of the public credentials.
     9	# - getblockfrompeer: lets the caller drive the node's P2P block fetching.
    10	# - fundrawtransaction: operates on a loaded bitcoind wallet — it selects and
    11	#   locks UTXOs and reveals change/wallet contents, violating the wallet-safety
    12	#   bar above. No local consumer (lnd, electrs, nbxplorer) uses it; lnd/nbxplorer
    13	#   manage their own wallets and electrs only reads blocks. Removed in the audit
    14	#   follow-up 2026-08-20.
    15	# If you need any of these, grant them via
    16	# `services.bitcoind.rpc.users.public.rpcwhitelist` in your own config.
    17	#
    18	# NOTE: getpeerinfo / getnodeaddresses stay in the list. This "public" user
    19	# is also the default RPC user that LOCAL services use, and lnd REQUIRES both
    20	# (its chain-backend health check + pruned-mode block fetching) — removing
    21	# them makes lnd fail its health check and shut itself down. They only leak
    22	# peer topology if you actually expose this user over a public proxy; if you
    23	# do that, narrow the whitelist in your own config rather than here.
    24	[
    25	  "echo"
    26	  "getinfo"
    27	  "getindexinfo"

    14	  # This is safe because all containing files have their specific private
    15	  # permissions set.
    16	  systemd.services.allowSecretsDirAccess = {
    17	    requires = [ "keys.target" ];
    18	    after = [ "keys.target" ];
    19	    script = "chmod o+x ${config.nix-bitcoin.secretsDir}";
    20	    serviceConfig.Type = "oneshot";
    21	  };
    22	
    23	  systemd.targets.nix-bitcoin-secrets = {
    24	    requires = [ "allowSecretsDirAccess.service" ];
    25	    after = [ "allowSecretsDirAccess.service" ];
    26	  };

     1	# vulnix whitelist for the repo CVE scan (test/ci/cve-scan.sh).
     2	#
     3	# Each entry (a [pname] section) silences findings that are false positives, not
     4	# applicable to how we ship the package, or accepted with a documented reason.
     5	# An entry with no `cve` key whitelists all of that package's current and future
     6	# CVEs — so keep entries NARROW and justified. Format:
     7	# https://github.com/flyingcircusio/vulnix
     8	#
     9	# The scan target (test/ci/scan-node.nix) is a representative node's runtime
    10	# closure. That closure still drags in the BUILD toolchain of some services
    11	# (compilers, build systems, VCS used to fetch sources). Those packages are
    12	# never invoked by a running node service and are not network-reachable, so
    13	# their CVEs are not node attack surface. They are whitelisted below. Everything
    14	# else — glibc, zlib, sqlite, dbus, nghttp2, snappy, openssl, and the like — is
    15	# left REPORTED for real human triage (is our version actually affected? is
    16	# there an upstream fix to pull into pkgs/overrides.nix?).
    17	#
    18	# Reviewed 2026-08-20. Revisit if a service starts invoking one of these at
    19	# runtime (then it becomes attack surface again).
    20	#
    21	# The entries above (build tooling) whitelist a whole pname. The section at the
    22	# BOTTOM is a different category: vulnix pname COLLISIONS — CVEs that belong to
    23	# an unrelated product sharing the package name, and do not apply to the software
    24	# we ship at all. Those are scoped with an explicit `cve = [...]` list (never a
    25	# blanket pname whitelist), so a genuinely-new CVE for the real package still
    26	# reports. Each was verified against NVD on the date noted.
    27	#
    28	# Reviewed 2026-08-24 (collision triage of cve-scan issue #1).
    29

     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# cve-scan.sh — scan the fork's shipped service closure for known CVEs.
     3	#
     4	# We ship NixOS MODULES, not a flat package set, so the honest scan target is a
     5	# built node's RUNTIME closure. test/ci/scan-node.nix builds a representative
     6	# node with every shipped service enabled (bitcoind, lnd, electrs, btcpayserver,
     7	# nbxplorer) and we vulnix its system toplevel — the software a user actually
     8	# runs. (Scanning a test-derivation closure instead would include build-time
     9	# deps like compilers, whose CVEs never reach a deployed node.)
    10	#
    11	# vulnix matches every store path in that closure against the NVD. Findings not
    12	# covered by .github/cve-whitelist.toml are reported; the caller (workflow)
    13	# turns them into a tracking issue.
    14	#
    15	# Usage: ./test/ci/cve-scan.sh [scenario ...]   (default: default regtest)
    16	# Outputs (cwd): vulnix-report.json, cve-summary.md
    17	# Exit: 0 on a completed scan (findings are data, not a failure — the workflow
    18	# decides); nonzero on a FAILED scan (build/vulnix/NVD error), so a broken scan
    19	# fails the job loudly instead of masquerading as "clean" and closing the issue.
    20	set -euo pipefail
    21	
    22	cd "${BASH_SOURCE[0]%/*}/../.."
    23	REPO=$PWD
    24	WHITELIST="$REPO/.github/cve-whitelist.toml"
    25	NIX=(nix --extra-experimental-features "nix-command flakes")
    26	
    27	# Build a representative node's system toplevel and scan its RUNTIME closure —
    28	# the software a user actually runs. Scanning a test-derivation closure instead
    29	# would drag in build-time deps (go-bootstrap, cargo, shellcheck, …) whose CVEs
    30	# never reach a deployed node and would drown the real findings.
    31	echo "::group::build node toplevel"
    32	toplevel=$("${NIX[@]}" build --no-link --print-out-paths --impure -f test/ci/scan-node.nix) \
    33	  || { echo "node toplevel build failed"; echo '[]' > vulnix-report.json; : > cve-summary.md; exit 1; }
    34	echo "$toplevel"
    35	echo "::endgroup::"
    36	
    37	echo "::group::vulnix"
    38	# vulnix exits non-zero when it finds non-whitelisted vulns; that's expected,
    39	# not an error — capture the JSON regardless.
    40	set +e
    41	"${NIX[@]}" shell --inputs-from . nixpkgs#vulnix -c \
    42	  vulnix --json --whitelist "$WHITELIST" "$toplevel" > vulnix-report.json 2> vulnix.err
    43	rc=$?
    44	set -e
    45	cat vulnix.err || true
    46	echo "vulnix exit=$rc"
    47	echo "::endgroup::"
    48	
    49	# vulnix: 0 = clean, 2 = findings, 1 = scan error (NVD fetch, whitelist parse).
    50	# rc=2 is ALSO click's usage-error exit code, so additionally require the output
    51	# to be a JSON array — otherwise an error would read as a clean scan and the
    52	# workflow would close the tracking issue on garbage.
    53	if [ "$rc" -ne 0 ] && [ "$rc" -ne 2 ]; then
    54	  echo "vulnix scan FAILED (rc=$rc) — not reporting a result"; exit 1
    55	fi
    56	jq -e 'type == "array"' vulnix-report.json >/dev/null 2>&1 \
    57	  || { echo "vulnix produced no valid JSON (rc=$rc) — not reporting a result"; exit 1; }
    58	# Shape check on the entries too: a malformed element (e.g. affected_by not an
    59	# array) would make the counting expression below throw, and an error there
    60	# must never read as "0 findings" (second opinion 2026-09-09, low).
    61	jq -e 'all(.[]; type == "object" and ((.affected_by // []) | type == "array") and ((.whitelisted // []) | type == "array"))' vulnix-report.json >/dev/null 2>&1 \
    62	  || { echo "vulnix report entries have an unexpected shape — not reporting a result"; exit 1; }
    63	
    64	# Summarize with the runner's native jq (present on GitHub runners and pop-os).
    65	# vulnix --json fields: `affected_by` holds only the ACTIVE (non-whitelisted)
    66	# CVEs — whitelist-masked ones are moved to the `whitelisted` array, and
    67	# derivations whose CVEs are ALL masked are omitted from the JSON entirely.
    68	# So filter on affected_by alone: also requiring `whitelisted` to be empty
    69	# silently dropped any derivation carrying BOTH a scoped-whitelisted collision
    70	# CVE and a genuinely new one — defeating the per-CVE scoping policy.
    71	real='[.[] | select(((.affected_by // []) | length) > 0)]'
    72	maxcvss='(([.cvssv3_basescore // {} | .[]] | max) // 0)'
    73	count=$(jq "$real | length" vulnix-report.json) \
    74	  || { echo "cannot count findings in vulnix-report.json — not reporting a result"; exit 1; }
    75	[[ $count =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]] || { echo "non-numeric finding count '$count' — not reporting a result"; exit 1; }
    76	{
    77	  echo "# CVE scan — $(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%MZ)"
    78	  echo
    79	  echo "Scanned: representative node toplevel (secure-node preset: tor, operator; bitcoind + lnd + electrs + btcpayserver + nbxplorer + backups), runtime closure"
    80	  echo "Affected store paths with non-whitelisted CVEs: **$count**"
    81	  echo
    82	  if [ "$count" != 0 ]; then
    83	    # shellcheck disable=SC2016  # jq program, not shell — \(...) is jq syntax
    84	    jq -r "$real | map(. + {_m: $maxcvss}) | sort_by(-._m) | .[] | \"## \(.pname // .name) \(.version // \"\") — CVSS \(._m)\n- \((.affected_by // []) | join(\", \"))\"" \
    85	      vulnix-report.json
    86	    echo
    87	    echo "Triage: fix in \`pkgs/overrides.nix\` (version-guarded); whitelist only vulnix"
    88	    echo "pname collisions or CVEs that are N/A to this runtime closure in \`.github/cve-whitelist.toml\`"
    89	    echo "(this scans the node's RUNTIME closure, so build tooling should not appear — if it does, find out why)."
    90	  else
    91	    echo "No action needed."
    92	  fi
    93	} > cve-summary.md
    94	
    95	# Emit ONLY the machine-readable result to $GITHUB_OUTPUT (logs go to stdout).
    96	if [ "$count" != 0 ]; then found=true; else found=false; fi
    97	echo "found=$found" >> "${GITHUB_OUTPUT:-/dev/stdout}"
    98	echo "scan complete: found=$found"
     1	# Security-driven version overrides for packages whose pinned nixpkgs
     2	# version ships known vulnerabilities.
     3	#
     4	# Each override must be version-guarded so it turns inert once the pinned
     5	# nixpkgs catches up. Flake updates are then always safe to take.
     6	#
     7	# Example (from the clboss fund-theft fix, since resolved by
     8	# trimming clightning support from this fork):
     9	#
    10	#   clboss = if lagging pkgsUnstable.clboss "0.16.2" then pkgsUnstable.clboss.overrideAttrs (old: rec {
    11	#     version = "0.16.2";
    12	#     src = pkgs.fetchzip {
    13	#       url = "https://github.com/ZmnSCPxj/clboss/releases/download/v${version}/clboss-v${version}.tar.gz";
    14	#       hash = "...";
    15	#     };
    16	#   }) else pkgsUnstable.clboss;
    17	{ pkgs, pkgsUnstable }:
    18	let
    19	  inherit (pkgs) lib;
    20	  lagging = pkg: version: lib.versionOlder pkg.version version;
    21	in
    22	{
    23	  # 2.4.3 (2026-08-24) is a security release ("updating is recommended for
    24	  # servers shared with many users"); nixpkgs 26.05 carries 2.4.2. The nuget
    25	  # deps changed (HtmlSanitizer 9.1.982 -> 9.2.995 among others), so this is a
    26	  # full package copy with a regenerated lockfile (see ./btcpayserver/) —
    27	  # buildDotnetModule can't take nugetDeps via overrideAttrs. Drop the copy
    28	  # together with this override once the pin catches up.
    29	  btcpayserver = if lagging pkgs.btcpayserver "2.4.3"
    30	                 then pkgs.callPackage ./btcpayserver { }
    31	                 else pkgs.btcpayserver;
    32	}
     1	# Copy of nixpkgs' pkgs/by-name/bt/btcpayserver/package.nix, bumped to the
     2	# 2.4.3 security release (2026-08-24) with a regenerated nuget lockfile.
     3	# Wired up via a version-guarded entry in ../overrides.nix — buildDotnetModule
     4	# resolves `nugetDeps` from its ORIGINAL call args (and strips it from the
     5	# derivation attrs), so a plain overrideAttrs of version/src/nugetDeps cannot
     6	# work; re-calling the package function is the supported path.
     7	# Delete this directory together with the override once nixpkgs >= 2.4.3.
     8	{

    14	  name = "nix-bitcoin-environment";
    15	
    16	  helpMessage = ''
    17	    nix-bitcoin path: ${toString ../.}
    18	
    19	    Available commands
    20	    ==================
    21	    deploy
    22	      Run krops-deploy and eval-config in parallel.
    23	      This ensures that eval failures appear quickly when deploying.
    24	      In this case, deployment is stopped.
    25	
    26	    krops-deploy
    27	      Deploy your node via krops
    28	
    29	    eval-config
    30	      Evaluate your node system configuration
    31	
    32	    build-config
    33	       Build your node system on your local machine
    34	
    35	    generate-secrets
    36	      Create secrets required by your node configuration.
    37	      Secrets are written to ./secrets/
    38	      This function is automatically called by krops-deploy.
    39	
    40	    update-nix-bitcoin
    41	      Fetch and use the latest version of nix-bitcoin
    42	  '';

    44	  shellHook = ''
    45	    export NIX_PATH="nixpkgs=${nixpkgs}:nix-bitcoin=${toString ../.}:."
    46	    ${setPath}
    47	    export NIX_BITCOIN_EXAMPLES_DIR="${cfgDir}"
    48	    export nixpkgsUnstable="${pinned.nixpkgs-unstable}"
    49	
    50	    # Set isInteractive=1 if
    51	    # 1. stdout is a TTY, i.e. we're not piping the output
    52	    # 2. the shell is interactive
    53	    if [[ -t 1 && $- == *i* ]]; then isInteractive=1; else isInteractive=; fi
    54	
    55	    # Make this a non-environment var
    56	    export -n helpMessage
    57	
    58	    help() { echo "$helpMessage"; }
    59	    h() { help; }
    60	
    61	    fetch-release() {
    62	      ${toString ./fetch-release}
    63	    }
    64	
    65	    update-nix-bitcoin() {(
    66	      # Fork-native pin: resolve the fork's latest CalVer release tag to the
    67	      # commit it points at, prefetch that commit's tarball and rewrite
    68	      # nix-bitcoin-release.nix. Never fetches upstream fort-nix releases
    69	      # (they lack this fork's fixes). Tags are unsigned CI pointers — the
    70	      # commit is what you review; see SECURITY.md.
    71	      set -euo pipefail
    72	      releaseFile="${cfgDir}/nix-bitcoin-release.nix"
    73	      repo=https://github.com/ostermayer/nix-bitcoin
    74	      tag=$(git ls-remote --tags --refs "$repo" 'refs/tags/20*' | sed 's|.*refs/tags/||' | sort -V | tail -n1)
    75	      [[ $tag ]] || { echo "No release tag found on $repo" >&2; exit 1; }
    76	      commit=$(git ls-remote "$repo" "refs/tags/$tag^{}" | cut -f1)
    77	      [[ $commit ]] || commit=$(git ls-remote "$repo" "refs/tags/$tag" | cut -f1)
    78	      url="$repo/archive/$commit.tar.gz"
    79	      hash=$(nix-prefetch-url --unpack "$url" 2>/dev/null)
    80	      new=$(printf 'builtins.fetchTarball {\n  # fork release %s\n  url = "%s";\n  sha256 = "%s";\n}\n' "$tag" "$url" "$hash")
    81	      current=$(cat "$releaseFile" 2>/dev/null || true)
    82	      if [[ $new == "$current" ]]; then
    83	        echo "nix-bitcoin-release.nix already pins $tag ($commit)"
    84	      else
    85	        printf '%s' "$new" > "$releaseFile"
    86	        echo "Pinned $tag ($commit) in nix-bitcoin-release.nix — review that commit before deploying"
    87	        if [[ $isInteractive ]]; then
    88	          exec nix-shell
    89	        fi
    90	      fi
    91	    )}
    92	
    93	    generate-secrets() {(
    94	      set -euo pipefail
    95	      config="${cfgDir}/krops/krops-configuration.nix"
    96	      if [[ ! -e $config ]]; then
    97	        config="${cfgDir}/configuration.nix"
    98	      fi
    99	      genSecrets=$(nix-build --no-out-link -I nixos-config="$config" \
   100	                   '<nixpkgs/nixos>' -A config.nix-bitcoin.generateSecretsScript)
   101	      mkdir -p "${cfgDir}/secrets"
   102	      (cd "${cfgDir}/secrets"; $genSecrets)
   103	    )}
   104	
   105	    deploy() {(
   106	      set -euo pipefail
   107	      krops-deploy &
   108	      kropsPid=$!
   109	      if eval-config; then
   110	        wait $kropsPid
   111	      else
   112	        # Kill all subprocesses
   113	        kill $(pidClosure $kropsPid)
   114	        return 1
   115	      fi
   116	    )}
   117	
   118	    krops-deploy() {(
   119	      set -euo pipefail
   120	      generate-secrets
   121	      # Ensure strict permissions on secrets/ directory before rsyncing it to
   122	      # the target machine
   123	      chmod 700 "${cfgDir}/secrets"
   124	      $(nix-build --no-out-link "${cfgDir}/krops/deploy.nix")
   125	    )}
   126	
   127	    eval-config() {(
   128	      set -euo pipefail
   129	      system=$(getNodeSystem)
   130	      NIXOS_CONFIG="${cfgDir}/krops/krops-configuration.nix" \
   131	        nix-instantiate --eval ${nixpkgs}/nixos $system -A system.outPath | tr -d '"'
   132	      echo
   133	    )}
   134	
   135	    build-config() {(
   136	      set -euo pipefail
   137	      system=$(getNodeSystem)
   138	      NIXOS_CONFIG="${cfgDir}/krops/krops-configuration.nix" \
   139	        nix-build --no-out-link ${nixpkgs}/nixos $system -A system
   140	    )}
   141	
   142	    getNodeSystem() {
   143	      if [[ -e '${cfgDir}/krops/system' ]]; then
   144	        echo -n "--argstr system "; cat '${cfgDir}/krops/system'
   145	      elif [[ $OSTYPE == darwin* ]]; then
   146	        # On macOS, `builtins.currentSystem` (`*-darwin`) can never equal
   147	        # the node system (`*-linux`), so we can always provide a helpful error message:
   148	        >&2 echo "Error, node system not set. See here how to fix this:"
   149	        >&2 echo "https://github.com/fort-nix/nix-bitcoin/blob/master/docs/install.md#optional-specify-the-system-of-your-node"
   150	        return 1
   151	      fi
   152	    };
   153	
   154	    pidClosure() {
   155	      echo "$1"
   156	      for pid in $(ps -o pid= --ppid "$1"); do
   157	        pidClosure "$pid"
   158	      done
   159	    }
   160	
   161	    if [[ $isInteractive ]]; then
   162	      ${pkgs.figlet}/bin/figlet "nix-bitcoin"
   163	      echo 'Enter "h" or "help" for documentation.'
   164	    fi
   165	
   166	    # Don't run this hook when another nix-shell is run inside this shell
   167	    unset shellHook
   168	
   169	    ${extraShellInitCmds pkgs}
   170	  '';
   171	}

    16	
    17	# Strategy:
    18	# Job `build_test_drivers` evals all VM tests and builds all test drivers in a single Nix build.
    19	# Compared to launching a separate build job for each VM test, this avoids duplicated
    20	# builds of derivations that the test drivers have in common.
    21	#
    22	# The job matrix `test_scenario` runs the VM tests. Scenario jobs ALWAYS run —
    23	# build-to-cachix.sh early-exits green when a scenario's result is already in
    24	# the binary cache — so every push reports a real conclusion for all five
    25	# checks. (A job-level skip leaves the matrix contexts unreported, and the
    26	# release branch ruleset then blocks fast-forwards forever with
    27	# "required status checks are expected".)
    28	jobs:
    29	  build_test_drivers:
    30	    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    31	    steps:
    32	      - uses: actions/checkout@11d5960a326750d5838078e36cf38b85af677262 # v4
    33	      - uses: cachix/install-nix-action@b85815f71a6de0ddee80b8a80a98d43f4bcc66c7 # v31
    34	      - id: main
    35	        name: Build test drivers
    36	        run: |
    37	          echo "::group::Setup Nix env"
    38	          nix shell --inputs-from . nixpkgs#{bash,coreutils,cachix} -c ./test/ci/build_test_drivers.sh
    39	
    40	  test_scenario:
    41	    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    42	    strategy:
    43	      fail-fast: false
    44	      matrix:
    45	        # `default` (secureNode) and `regtest` boot the full stack
    46	        # (bitcoind + lnd + electrs + btcpayserver/nbxplorer) and assert it
    47	        # works — this catches RPC-whitelist / service-health regressions.
    48	        # `netns` covers network-namespace isolation.
    49	        scenario:
    50	          - default
    51	          - regtest
    52	          - netns
    53	    needs: build_test_drivers
    54	    steps:
    55	      - uses: actions/checkout@11d5960a326750d5838078e36cf38b85af677262 # v4
    56	      # VM tests run QEMU inside the Nix build sandbox and need /dev/kvm
    57	      # accessible to the build users. GitHub runners have KVM hardware; open
    58	      # its permissions so the sandbox can use it.
    59	      - name: Enable KVM
    60	        run: |
    61	          echo 'KERNEL=="kvm", GROUP="kvm", MODE="0666", OPTIONS+="static_node=kvm"' | sudo tee /etc/udev/rules.d/99-kvm4all.rules
    62	          sudo udevadm control --reload-rules
    63	          sudo udevadm trigger --name-match=kvm
    64	      - uses: cachix/install-nix-action@b85815f71a6de0ddee80b8a80a98d43f4bcc66c7 # v31
    65	      - run: nix shell --inputs-from . nixpkgs#{bash,coreutils,cachix} -c ./test/ci/build.sh ${{ matrix.scenario }}
    66

   388	    ];
   389	
   390	    systemd.services.bitcoind = rec {
   391	      wants = [
   392	        "network-online.target"
   393	        # Use `wants` instead of `requires` for `nix-bitcoin-secrets.target`
   394	        # so that bitcoind and all dependent services are not restarted when
   395	        # the secrets target restarts.
   396	        # The secrets target always restarts when deploying with one of the methods
   397	        # in ./deployment.
   398	        #
   399	        # TODO-EXTERNAL: Instead of `wants`, use a future systemd dependency type
   400	        # that propagates initial start failures but no restarts
   401	        "nix-bitcoin-secrets.target"
   402	      ];
   403	      after = wants;
   404	      wantedBy = [ "multi-user.target" ];
   405	
   406	      preStart = let
   407	        extraRpcauth = concatMapStrings (name: let
   408	          user = cfg.rpc.users.${name};
   409	        in optionalString user.passwordHMACFromFile ''
   410	            echo "rpcauth=${user.name}:$(cat ${secretsDir}/bitcoin-HMAC-${name})"
   411	          ''
   412	        ) (builtins.attrNames cfg.rpc.users);
   413	      in ''
   414	        ${optionalString cfg.dataDirReadableByGroup ''
   415	          if [[ -e '${cfg.dataDir}/blocks' ]]; then
   416	            chmod -R g+rX '${cfg.dataDir}/blocks'
   417	          fi
   418	        ''}
   419	
   420	        cfg=$(
   421	          cat ${configFile}
   422	          ${extraRpcauth}
   423	          echo
   424	          ${optionalString (cfg.getPublicAddressCmd != "") ''
   425	            echo "externalip=$(${cfg.getPublicAddressCmd})"
   426	          ''}
   427	        )
   428	        confFile='${cfg.dataDir}/bitcoin.conf'
   429	        if [[ ! -e $confFile || $cfg != $(cat $confFile) ]]; then
   430	          install -o '${cfg.user}' -g '${cfg.group}' -m 640 <(echo "$cfg") $confFile
   431	        fi
   432	      '';
   433	
   434	      # Enable RPC access for group
   435	      postStart = ''
   436	        # NOTE: this makes the RPC cookie readable by group `bitcoin` — every
   437	        # service in that group gets full privileged RPC. Keep group membership
   438	        # minimal; new services should use an rpc user + HMAC instead (audit L-10).
   439	        # Use an explicit 0640 rather than `chmod g=r`: the latter leaves the
   440	        # other-read bit at whatever bitcoind created (its umask), so the cookie's
   441	        # world-readability would hinge on the app's file mode. 0640 clears it.
   442	        chmod 0640 '${cfg.dataDir}/${optionalString cfg.regtest "regtest/"}.cookie'
   443	      '' + (optionalString cfg.regtest) ''
   444	        chmod g=x '${cfg.dataDir}/regtest'
   445	      '';
   446	
   447	      serviceConfig = nbLib.defaultHardening // {
   448	        Type = "notify";
   449	        NotifyAccess = "all";
   450	        User = cfg.user;
   451	        Group = cfg.group;
   452	        TimeoutStartSec = "30min";
   453	        TimeoutStopSec = "30min";
   454	        ExecStart = "${cfg.package}/bin/bitcoind -datadir='${cfg.dataDir}'";
   455	        Restart = "on-failure";
   456	        UMask = mkIf cfg.dataDirReadableByGroup "0027";
   457	        ReadWritePaths = [ cfg.dataDir ];
   458	      } // nbLib.allowedIPAddresses cfg.tor.enforce
   459	        // optionalAttrs zmqServerEnabled nbLib.allowNetlink;
   460	    };
   461	
   462	    users.users.${cfg.user} = {
   463	      isSystemUser = true;
   464	      group = cfg.group;
   465	    };
   466	    users.groups.${cfg.group} = {};
   467	    users.groups.bitcoinrpc-public = {};

   469	    nix-bitcoin.operator.groups = [ cfg.group ];

   471	    nix-bitcoin.secrets = {

   473	      bitcoin-rpcpassword-public = {

   475	        group = "bitcoinrpc-public";
   476	      };
   477	
   478	      bitcoin-HMAC-privileged.user = cfg.user;
   479	      bitcoin-HMAC-public.user = cfg.user;
   480	    };
   481	    nix-bitcoin.generateSecretsCmds.bitcoind = ''
   482	      makeBitcoinRPCPassword privileged
   483	      makeBitcoinRPCPassword public
   484	    '';
   485	  };
   486	}

     1	# Security-driven version overrides for packages whose pinned nixpkgs
     2	# version ships known vulnerabilities.
     3	#
     4	# Each override must be version-guarded so it turns inert once the pinned
     5	# nixpkgs catches up. Flake updates are then always safe to take.
     6	#
     7	# Example (from the clboss fund-theft fix, since resolved by
     8	# trimming clightning support from this fork):
     9	#
    10	#   clboss = if lagging pkgsUnstable.clboss "0.16.2" then pkgsUnstable.clboss.overrideAttrs (old: rec {
    11	#     version = "0.16.2";
    12	#     src = pkgs.fetchzip {
    13	#       url = "https://github.com/ZmnSCPxj/clboss/releases/download/v${version}/clboss-v${version}.tar.gz";
    14	#       hash = "...";
    15	#     };
    16	#   }) else pkgsUnstable.clboss;
    17	{ pkgs, pkgsUnstable }:
    18	let
    19	  inherit (pkgs) lib;
    20	  lagging = pkg: version: lib.versionOlder pkg.version version;
    21	in
    22	{
    23	  # 2.4.3 (2026-08-24) is a security release ("updating is recommended for
    24	  # servers shared with many users"); nixpkgs 26.05 carries 2.4.2. The nuget
    25	  # deps changed (HtmlSanitizer 9.1.982 -> 9.2.995 among others), so this is a
    26	  # full package copy with a regenerated lockfile (see ./btcpayserver/) —
    27	  # buildDotnetModule can't take nugetDeps via overrideAttrs. Drop the copy
    28	  # together with this override once the pin catches up.
    29	  btcpayserver = if lagging pkgs.btcpayserver "2.4.3"
    30	                 then pkgs.callPackage ./btcpayserver { }
    31	                 else pkgs.btcpayserver;
    32	}
     1	# Copy of nixpkgs' pkgs/by-name/bt/btcpayserver/package.nix, bumped to the
     2	# 2.4.3 security release (2026-08-24) with a regenerated nuget lockfile.
     3	# Wired up via a version-guarded entry in ../overrides.nix — buildDotnetModule
     4	# resolves `nugetDeps` from its ORIGINAL call args (and strips it from the
     5	# derivation attrs), so a plain overrideAttrs of version/src/nugetDeps cannot
     6	# work; re-calling the package function is the supported path.
     7	# Delete this directory together with the override once nixpkgs >= 2.4.3.
     8	{
     9	  lib,
    10	  buildDotnetModule,
    11	  fetchFromGitHub,
    12	  dotnetCorePackages,
    13	  altcoinSupport ? false,
    14	}:

    32

    34

     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	# cve-scan.sh — scan the fork's shipped service closure for known CVEs.
     3	#
     4	# We ship NixOS MODULES, not a flat package set, so the honest scan target is a
     5	# built node's RUNTIME closure. test/ci/scan-node.nix builds a representative
     6	# node with every shipped service enabled (bitcoind, lnd, electrs, btcpayserver,
     7	# nbxplorer) and we vulnix its system toplevel — the software a user actually
     8	# runs. (Scanning a test-derivation closure instead would include build-time
     9	# deps like compilers, whose CVEs never reach a deployed node.)
    10	#
    11	# vulnix matches every store path in that closure against the NVD. Findings not
    12	# covered by .github/cve-whitelist.toml are reported; the caller (workflow)
    13	# turns them into a tracking issue.
    14	#
    15	# Usage: ./test/ci/cve-scan.sh [scenario ...]   (default: default regtest)
    16	# Outputs (cwd): vulnix-report.json, cve-summary.md
    17	# Exit: 0 on a completed scan (findings are data, not a failure — the workflow
    18	# decides); nonzero on a FAILED scan (build/vulnix/NVD error), so a broken scan
    19	# fails the job loudly instead of masquerading as "clean" and closing the issue.
    20	set -euo pipefail
    21	
    22	cd "${BASH_SOURCE[0]%/*}/../.."
    23	REPO=$PWD
    24	WHITELIST="$REPO/.github/cve-whitelist.toml"
    25	NIX=(nix --extra-experimental-features "nix-command flakes")
    26	
    27	# Build a representative node's system toplevel and scan its RUNTIME closure —
    28	# the software a user actually runs. Scanning a test-derivation closure instead
    29	# would drag in build-time deps (go-bootstrap, cargo, shellcheck, …) whose CVEs
    30	# never reach a deployed node and would drown the real findings.
    31	echo "::group::build node toplevel"
    32	toplevel=$("${NIX[@]}" build --no-link --print-out-paths --impure -f test/ci/scan-node.nix) \
    33	  || { echo "node toplevel build failed"; echo '[]' > vulnix-report.json; : > cve-summary.md; exit 1; }
    34	echo "$toplevel"
    35	echo "::endgroup::"
    36	
    37	echo "::group::vulnix"
    38	# vulnix exits non-zero when it finds non-whitelisted vulns; that's expected,
    39	# not an error — capture the JSON regardless.
    40	set +e
    41	"${NIX[@]}" shell --inputs-from . nixpkgs#vulnix -c \
    42	  vulnix --json --whitelist "$WHITELIST" "$toplevel" > vulnix-report.json 2> vulnix.err
    43	rc=$?
    44	set -e
    45	cat vulnix.err || true
    46	echo "vulnix exit=$rc"
    47	echo "::endgroup::"
    48	
    49	# vulnix: 0 = clean, 2 = findings, 1 = scan error (NVD fetch, whitelist parse).
    50	# rc=2 is ALSO click's usage-error exit code, so additionally require the output
    51	# to be a JSON array — otherwise an error would read as a clean scan and the
    52	# workflow would close the tracking issue on garbage.
    53	if [ "$rc" -ne 0 ] && [ "$rc" -ne 2 ]; then
    54	  echo "vulnix scan FAILED (rc=$rc) — not reporting a result"; exit 1
    55	fi
    56	jq -e 'type == "array"' vulnix-report.json >/dev/null 2>&1 \
    57	  || { echo "vulnix produced no valid JSON (rc=$rc) — not reporting a result"; exit 1; }
    58	# Shape check on the entries too: a malformed element (e.g. affected_by not an
    59	# array) would make the counting expression below throw, and an error there
    60	# must never read as "0 findings" (second opinion 2026-09-09, low).
    61	jq -e 'all(.[]; type == "object" and ((.affected_by // []) | type == "array") and ((.whitelisted // []) | type == "array"))' vulnix-report.json >/dev/null 2>&1 \
    62	  || { echo "vulnix report entries have an unexpected shape — not reporting a result"; exit 1; }
    63	
    64	# Summarize with the runner's native jq (present on GitHub runners and pop-os).
    65	# vulnix --json fields: `affected_by` holds only the ACTIVE (non-whitelisted)
    66	# CVEs — whitelist-masked ones are moved to the `whitelisted` array, and
    67	# derivations whose CVEs are ALL masked are omitted from the JSON entirely.
    68	# So filter on affected_by alone: also requiring `whitelisted` to be empty
    69	# silently dropped any derivation carrying BOTH a scoped-whitelisted collision
    70	# CVE and a genuinely new one — defeating the per-CVE scoping policy.
    71	real='[.[] | select(((.affected_by // []) | length) > 0)]'
    72	maxcvss='(([.cvssv3_basescore // {} | .[]] | max) // 0)'
    73	count=$(jq "$real | length" vulnix-report.json) \
    74	  || { echo "cannot count findings in vulnix-report.json — not reporting a result"; exit 1; }
    75	[[ $count =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]] || { echo "non-numeric finding count '$count' — not reporting a result"; exit 1; }
    76	{
    77	  echo "# CVE scan — $(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%MZ)"
    78	  echo
    79	  echo "Scanned: representative node toplevel (secure-node preset: tor, operator; bitcoind + lnd + electrs + btcpayserver + nbxplorer + backups), runtime closure"
    80	  echo "Affected store paths with non-whitelisted CVEs: **$count**"
    81	  echo
    82	  if [ "$count" != 0 ]; then
    83	    # shellcheck disable=SC2016  # jq program, not shell — \(...) is jq syntax
    84	    jq -r "$real | map(. + {_m: $maxcvss}) | sort_by(-._m) | .[] | \"## \(.pname // .name) \(.version // \"\") — CVSS \(._m)\n- \((.affected_by // []) | join(\", \"))\"" \

    86	    echo
    87	    echo "Triage: fix in \`pkgs/overrides.nix\` (version-guarded); whitelist only vulnix"
    88	    echo "pname collisions or CVEs that are N/A to this runtime closure in \`.github/cve-whitelist.toml\`"
    89	    echo "(this scans the node's RUNTIME closure, so build tooling should not appear — if it does, find out why)."
    90	  else
    91	    echo "No action needed."
    92	  fi
    93	} > cve-summary.md
    94

    96	if [ "$count" != 0 ]; then found=true; else found=false; fi

     1	# Representative nix-bitcoin node: the secure-node preset plus every shipped
     2	# service and backups — i.e. the recommended deployment.
     3	#
     4	# CVE-scan target (test/ci/cve-scan.sh): vulnix scans this system's RUNTIME
     5	# closure — the software a user actually runs (bitcoind, lnd, electrs,
     6	# btcpayserver, nbxplorer, kernel, systemd, openssl, …) — NOT build-time
     7	# dependencies like compilers, which are what a test-derivation closure drags
     8	# in and are irrelevant to a deployed node.
     9	#

    13	  lib = f.inputs.nixpkgs.lib;
    14	in

    16	  system = "x86_64-linux";
    17	  modules = [
    18	    f.nixosModules.default
    19	    # The recommended deployment, not the bare service set: secure-node pulls
    20	    # in tor (client + onion services — the largest continuously exposed
    21	    # parsing surface on a real node), the operator/doas setup and the preset
    22	    # infrastructure; backups adds duplicity. Without these the scan silently
    23	    # skipped exactly the components a deployed node exposes (audit

    25	    "${f.outPath}/modules/presets/secure-node.nix"

     1	#!/usr/bin/env bash
     2	
     3	# Modules integration test runner.
     4	# The tests (defined in ./tests.nix) use the NixOS testing framework and are executed in a VM.
     5	#
     6	# Usage:
     7	#   Run the basic set of tests. These are also run on the CI server.
     8	#   ./run-tests.sh
     9	#
    10	#   Test specific scenario
    11	#   ./run-tests.sh --scenario|-s <scenario>
    12	#
    13	#   - When <scenario> contains a space, <scenario> is treated as nix code defining
    14	#     a scenario. It is evaluated in the same context as other scenarios in ./tests.nix
    15	#
    16	#     Example:
    17	#     ./run-tests.sh -s "{ nix-bitcoin.nodeinfo.enable = true; }" container --run c nodeinfo
    18	#
    19	#   - When <scenario> does not name a scenario, the test is run with an adhoc scenario
    20	#     where services.<scenario> is enabled.
    21	#
    22	#     Example:
    23	#     ./run-tests.sh -s electrs
    24	#
    25	#   Run test(s) and link results to avoid garbage collection
    26	#   ./run-tests.sh [--scenario <scenario>] --out-link-prefix /tmp/nix-bitcoin-test
    27	#
    28	#   Pass extra args to nix-build
    29	#   ./run-tests.sh build --builders 'ssh://mybuildhost - - 15'
    30	#
    31	#   Run interactive test debugging
    32	#   ./run-tests.sh [--scenario <scenario>] debug
    33	#
    34	#     This starts the testing VM and drops you into a Python REPL where you can
    35	#     manually execute the tests from ./tests.py
    36	#
    37	#   Run a test scenario in a container
    38	#   sudo ./run-tests.sh [--scenario <scenario>] container
    39	#
    40	#     This is useful for quick experiments; containers start much faster than VMs.
    41	#     Running the Python test suite in containers is not yet supported.
    42	#     For now, creating NixOS containers requires root permissions.
    43	#     See ./lib/make-container.sh for a complete documentation.
    44	#
    45	#   Run a test scenario in a regular NixOS VM.
    46	#   No tests are executed, the machine's serial console is attached to your terminal.
    47	#   ./run-tests.sh [--scenario <scenario>] vm
    48	#
    49	#     This is useful for directly exploring a test configuration without the
    50	#     intermediate Python REPL layer.
    51	#     Run command 'q' inside the machine for instant poweroff.
    52	#
    53	#   Run tests from a snapshot copy of the source files
    54	#   ./run-tests.sh --copy-src|-c ...
    55	#
    56	#     This allows you to continue editing the nix-bitcoin sources while tests are running
    57	#     and reading source files.
    58	#     Files are copied to /tmp, a caching scheme helps minimizing copies.
    59	#
    60	#   Add custom scenarios from a file
    61	#   ./run-tests.sh --extra-scenarios ~/my/scenarios.nix ...

     9	# Cleanup on exit
    10	cleanup() {
    11	    set +eu
    12	    if [[ $qemuPID ]]; then
    13	        kill -9 "$qemuPID"
    14	    fi
    15	    rm -rf "$tmpDir"
    16	}
    17	trap "cleanup" EXIT
    18	
    19	identityFile=$qemuDir/id-vm
    20	chmod 0600 "$identityFile"
    21	
    22	runVM() {
    23	    vm=$1
    24	    vmNumCPUs=$2
    25	    vmMemoryMiB=$3
    26	    sshPort=$4
    27	
    28	    export NIX_DISK_IMAGE="$tmpDir/img"
    29	    export QEMU_NET_OPTS="hostfwd=tcp::${sshPort}-:22"
    30	    # shellcheck disable=SC2211
    31	    </dev/null "$vm"/bin/run-*-vm -m "$vmMemoryMiB" -smp "$vmNumCPUs" &>/dev/null &
    32	    qemuPID=$!
    33	}
    34	
    35	vmWaitForSSH() {
    36	    echo
    37	    printf "Waiting for SSH connection..."
    38	    waitUntil "c : 2>/dev/null" 500
    39	    echo
    40	}
    41	
    42	# Run command in VM
    43	c() {
    44	    ssh -p "$sshPort" -i "$identityFile" -o IdentitiesOnly=yes -o ConnectTimeout=1 \
    45	        -o StrictHostKeyChecking=no -o UserKnownHostsFile=/dev/null -o LogLevel=ERROR \
    46	        -o ControlMaster=auto -o ControlPath=$tmpDir/ssh-connection -o ControlPersist=60 \
    47	        root@127.0.0.1 "$@"
    48	}
    49	export identityFile
    50	export sshPort
    51	export tmpDir
    52	export -f c

     1	# Deferred: bind lnd's REST API to the WireGuard address
     2	
     3	Status: **DEFERRED — do not implement without working through every item below.**
     4	
     5	The 2026-08-24 adversarial audit (run `2026-08-24-111031`, both models) flagged
     6	that the wireguard preset sets `services.lnd.restAddress = "0.0.0.0"`
     7	(modules/presets/wireguard.nix), exposing the macaroon-admin REST API on all
     8	interfaces and relying solely on the nixos-fw firewall (default-drop plus an
     9	accept rule for the wg subnet) for protection. Severity medium/low, advisory.
    10	The suggested fix — bind to the wg server address (`${subnet}.1`, default
    11	10.10.0.1) instead — is correct as defense-in-depth but **breaks several
    12	things that are non-obvious**, because of one load-bearing detail:
    13	
    14	> `nbLib.address` (pkgs/lib.nix) maps the bind address `0.0.0.0` to the
    15	> *connect* address `127.0.0.1`. Every internal consumer of `restAddress`
    16	> goes through it. Today they all silently talk to localhost, which the
    17	> default TLS cert covers. Bind to `10.10.0.1` and they **all** follow.
    18	
    19	## What a naive `restAddress = serverAddress` breaks
    20	
    21	1. **lnd startup becomes hard-coupled to WireGuard.** lnd fails fatally if it
    22	   cannot bind `restlisten`; the `10.10.0.1` address only exists once
    23	   `wireguard-wg-nb.service` has configured the interface. Without systemd
    24	   ordering, boot is a crash-loop race (`Restart=on-failure`/10s masks it
    25	   until it doesn't). *With* ordering, a broken wg setup (e.g. missing
    26	   `wg-server-private-key` secret) takes the whole lightning node down —
    27	   today lnd runs fine with wg broken. This coupling is inherent to the fix;
    28	   it must be a conscious acceptance, or defused with
    29	   `net.ipv4.ip_nonlocal_bind = 1` (system-wide sysctl loosening — an
    30	   auditor trade-off of its own).
    31	
    32	2. **TLS cert SANs.** The generated lnd cert only has
    33	   `DNS:localhost,IP:127.0.0.1` plus `rpcAddress` when non-local
    34	   (modules/secrets/secrets.nix `doMakeCert`; modules/lnd.nix
    35	   `certificate.extraIPs`). Nothing adds the REST address. Any validating
    36	   client connecting to `https://10.10.0.1:8080` fails TLS. The preset must
    37	   set `services.lnd.certificate.extraIPs = [ serverAddress ]`. Note
    38	   `makeCert` *does* regenerate the cert when alt-names change
    39	   (secrets.nix, `$name-cert-alt-names` comparison), so deployed nodes pick
    40	   the new SAN up on the next deploy — but anything that pinned the old cert
    41	   must tolerate rotation.
    42	
    43	3. **Macaroon creation runs in lnd's `ExecStartPost`** (modules/lnd.nix): a
    44	   root script curls `https://<nbLib.address restAddress>:<restPort>/v1` with
    45	   `--cacert`. It would move from 127.0.0.1 to 10.10.0.1 → fails without
    46	   item 2 and item 1 → **lnd.service itself fails post-start**, even though
    47	   the daemon is healthy.
    48	
    49	4. **btcpayserver's lnd-rest backend** (modules/btcpayserver.nix,
    50	   `btclightning=...server=https://<address restAddress>...`) moves to
    51	   10.10.0.1 too: needs the SAN fix and makes btcpayserver transitively
    52	   wg-dependent.
    53	
    54	5. **lndconnect onion mode** (modules/lndconnect.nix): the Tor hidden
    55	   service forwards to `nbLib.address lnd.restAddress`. Tor-based mobile
    56	   access would then depend on the wg interface being up.
    57	
    58	6. **nodeinfo** (modules/nodeinfo.nix) reports `rest_address` — cosmetic,
    59	   but scripts parsing it see a new value.
    60	
    61	## Recommended design (avoids 2–6 entirely)
    62	
    63	Do **not** touch `restAddress`. Leave it at its default `127.0.0.1` and have
    64	the preset add a *second* listener — lnd accepts repeated `restlisten`
    65	entries:
    66	
    67	```nix
    68	services.lnd.extraConfig = ''
    69	  restlisten=${serverAddress}:${toString lnd.restPort}
    70	'';
    71	```
    72	
    73	All internal consumers keep localhost (valid cert SAN, no wg coupling); the
    74	wg peer reaches `10.10.0.1:8080` exactly as before (`lndconnect-wg` already
    75	targets `serverAddress` with `--nocert`). Only item 1 — the bind-time
    76	dependency on the interface — remains, and still needs:
    77	
    78	- `systemd.services.lnd = { wants = [ "wireguard-wg-nb.service" ]; after = [ "wireguard-wg-nb.service" ]; }`
    79	  in the preset (runtime wg restarts are fine: a bound socket survives
    80	  interface teardown and resumes when the address returns);
    81	- the firewall accept rule stays (nixos-fw default-drops; the rule is what
    82	  admits the peer). Since 2026-09-09 it is already scoped to the interface
    83	  and the single peer (`-i wg-nb -s <peerAddress>/32`), closing the
    84	  2026-08-29 low: a spoofed-source packet on the external interface no
    85	  longer matches the rule at all, independent of rp_filter/routing.
    86	
    87	## Pre-flight checklist before shipping any variant
    88	
    89	- [ ] Decide on the lnd↔wg startup coupling (accept, or `ip_nonlocal_bind`).
    90	- [ ] `test/wireguard-lndconnect.nix`: assert lnd comes up cleanly from a
    91	      cold boot (ordering race), REST reachable over wg at
    92	      `serverAddress:restPort`, REST **not** bound on the external
    93	      interface (the actual point of the fix), macaroon `ExecStartPost`
    94	      succeeded, and — if btcpayserver is in the scenario — its lnd backend
    95	      connects.
    96	- [ ] Full local VM suite green (`./test/run-tests.sh`).
    97	- [ ] Verify on a staging deploy that cert regeneration (if `restAddress`
    98	      was changed rather than the extraConfig variant) does not strand
    99	      existing clients.
   100	- [ ] Re-run the adversarial LLM audit (audits branch) — per policy every
   101	      fix re-runs the whole review; the review should specifically re-check
   102	      items 1–6.
     1	# Security Policy
     2	
     3	This repository is an independently maintained fork of
     4	[fort-nix/nix-bitcoin](https://github.com/fort-nix/nix-bitcoin), which is no
     5	longer actively maintained. The contacts, GPG keys, signing key, and security
     6	fund listed in upstream's SECURITY.md belong to the original developers and do
     7	not reach or represent this fork. Do not send reports or donations to them on
     8	our behalf.
     9	
    10	## Reporting a Vulnerability
    11	
    12	Report vulnerabilities through [GitHub private vulnerability
    13	reporting](../../security/advisories/new). This opens a private security
    14	advisory that only the maintainers can see.
    15	
    16	If private reporting is unavailable, open a regular issue that says you have a
    17	security concern, without any details, and we will arrange a private channel.
    18	
    19	Please include:
    20	
    21	- The component and version or commit you tested
    22	- Steps to reproduce or a proof of concept
    23	- The impact you see (fund theft, privacy loss, denial of service, ...)
    24	
    25	We aim to acknowledge reports within 7 days. We follow coordinated disclosure:
    26	we agree on a public disclosure date with you, credit you in the fix and
    27	release notes unless you prefer anonymity.
    28	
    29	## Scope
    30	
    31	In scope:
    32	
    33	- nix-bitcoin's own code: NixOS modules, `netns-exec`, secrets handling,
    34	  helper scripts, packaging in `pkgs/`
    35	- Privilege boundaries we claim to provide: service isolation, RPC
    36	  whitelisting, `netns-isolation`, systemd sandboxing
    37	- Documentation that instructs users to do something blatantly insecure
    38	
    39	Out of scope:
    40	
    41	- Vulnerabilities in the daemons we package (bitcoind, lnd,
    42	  electrs, btcpayserver, nbxplorer, ...). Report those to the upstream
    43	  project first. If upstream has shipped a fix that we have not yet pulled in,
    44	  tell us and we will treat it as urgent.
    45	- Attacks requiring physical access or an already-compromised deployment
    46	  machine.
    47	
    48	## Supported Versions
    49	
    50	Only the latest commit on the default branch and the latest release are
    51	supported. Security fixes are delivered by updating your flake input; we do
    52	not maintain stable branches.
    53	
    54	## Security Tracker
    55	
    56	Known findings and their status are tracked in
    57	[docs/security-audit-2026-08-14.md](docs/security-audit-2026-08-14.md). The
    58	audit is re-run on every nixpkgs pin update.
    59	
    60	## Adversarial review
    61	
    62	Beyond CI's NixOS VM assertion suite, every change is reviewed by two
    63	independent open-weight models (Kimi K3 + GLM-5.2) acting as adversarial
    64	security auditors, at max reasoning. The **methodology, the audit tooling, and
    65	the full results of every run** live on the [`audits`](../../tree/audits)
    66	branch — kept off `master`/`release` so the code you pin stays clean. Browse it
    67	to follow the reviews, read the raw transcripts, or run the suite yourself;
    68	PRs to improve the audit prompt are welcome.
    69	
    70	## Release Integrity
    71	
    72	Commits on this fork are signed with the maintainer's SSH key. GitHub shows
    73	them as "Verified"; to verify locally, add the maintainer's public signing key
    74	to an SSH allowed-signers file and run `git log --show-signature` or
    75	`git verify-commit <rev>`. The CalVer release tags are created by CI
    76	(`release-tag.yml`) as annotated but **unsigned** tags — a tag name is a
    77	convenience pointer, not a signature. Verify the *commit* a tag points at, and
    78	pin by commit hash + `narHash` (below) rather than by tag name.
    79	
    80	Because this fork is consumed as a flake input, integrity ultimately comes
    81	from your `flake.lock`: it records the exact commit and its content hash
    82	(`narHash`), and Nix refuses any input whose contents do not match. Pin a
    83	specific revision and review changes before advancing it.
    84	
    85	`bitcoind` source tarballs are additionally verified against the Bitcoin
    86	Core `guix.sigs` keyring at build time.
    87	
    88	Note: the upstream tarball-release helper `helper/fetch-release` is not used by
    89	this fork. It fetches upstream fort-nix releases and references the original
    90	maintainer's GPG key. It refuses to run unless `NIX_BITCOIN_FETCH_UPSTREAM=1`
    91	is set, and it points at the flake pin instead. Do not rely on it. This fork
    92	releases through CalVer tags on the `release` branch (`release-tag.yml`).

    72	
    73	    generateSecretsScript = mkOption {
    74	      internal = true;
    75	      default = let
    76	        rpcauthSrc = pkgs.fetchurl {
    77	          url = "https://raw.githubusercontent.com/bitcoin/bitcoin/d6cde007db9d3e6ee93bd98a9bbfdce9bfa9b15b/share/rpcauth/rpcauth.py";
    78	          sha256 = "189mpplam6yzizssrgiyv70c9899ggh8cac76j4n7v0xqzfip07n";
    79	        };
    80	        # rpcauth.py takes the password as an argv element, which is
    81	        # world-readable via /proc/<pid>/cmdline while the process runs
    82	        # (audit L-6). Pass it via file instead.
    83	        rpcauth = pkgs.writers.writeBash "rpcauth" ''
    84	          exec ${pkgs.python3}/bin/python -c '
    85	          import runpy, sys
    86	          with open(sys.argv[2]) as f:
    87	              password = f.read().strip()
    88	          sys.argv = ["rpcauth.py", sys.argv[1], password]
    89	          runpy.run_path("${rpcauthSrc}", run_name="__main__")
    90	          ' "$@"
    91	        '';
    92	      # Writes secrets to PWD
    93	      in pkgs.writers.writeBash "generate-secrets" ''
    94	        set -euo pipefail
    95	
    96	        export PATH=${lib.makeBinPath (with pkgs; [ coreutils gnugrep ])}
    97	
    98	        makePasswordSecret() {
    99	          # Passwords have alphabet {a-z, A-Z, 0-9} and ~119 bits of entropy
   100	          [[ -e $1 ]] || ${pkgs.pwgen}/bin/pwgen -s 20 1 > "$1"
   101	        }
   102	        makeBitcoinRPCPassword() {
   103	          user=$1
   104	          file=bitcoin-rpcpassword-$user
   105	          HMACfile=bitcoin-HMAC-$user
   106	          makePasswordSecret "$file"
   107	          if [[ $file -nt $HMACfile ]]; then
   108	            ${rpcauth} "$user" "$file" | grep rpcauth | cut -d ':' -f 2 > "$HMACfile"
   109	          fi
   110	        }
   111	        makeCert() {
   112	          name=$1
   113	          # Add leading comma if not empty
   114	          extraAltNames=''${2:+,}''${2:-}
   115	          if [[ ! -e $name-key ]]; then
   116	            # Create new key and cert
   117	            doMakeCert "-newkey ec -pkeyopt ec_paramgen_curve:prime256v1 -nodes -keyout $name-key"
   118	          elif [[ ! -e $name-cert \
   119	                  || $(cat "$name-cert-alt-names" 2>/dev/null) != $extraAltNames ]]; then
   120	            # Create cert from existing key
   121	            doMakeCert "-key $name-key"
   122	          fi;
   123	        }
   124	        doMakeCert() {
   125	           # This fn uses global variables `name` and `extraAltNames`
   126	           keyOpts=$1
   127	           ${pkgs.openssl}/bin/openssl req -x509 \
   128	             -sha256 -days 3650 $keyOpts -out "$name-cert"  \
   129	             -subj "/CN=localhost/O=$name" \
   130	             -addext "subjectAltName=DNS:localhost,IP:127.0.0.1$extraAltNames"
   131	           echo "$extraAltNames" > "$name-cert-alt-names"
   132	        }
   133	
   134	        umask u=rw,go=
   135	        ${builtins.concatStringsSep "\n" (builtins.attrValues cfg.generateSecretsCmds)}
   136	      '';
   137	    };
   138	  };
   139	
   140	  cfg = config.nix-bitcoin;
   141	in {
   142	  inherit options;
   143	
   144	  config = {
   145	    assertions = [
   146	      { assertion = cfg.secretsSetupMethod != null;
   147	        message = ''
   148	          No secrets setup method has been defined.
   149	          To fix this, choose one of the following:
   150	
   151	           - Use one of the deployment methods in ${toString ./../deployment}
   152	
   153	           - Set `nix-bitcoin.generateSecrets = true` to automatically generate secrets
   154	
   155	           - Set `nix-bitcoin.secretsSetupMethod = "manual"` if you want to manually setup secrets
   156	        '';
   157	      }
   158	    ];
   159	
   160	    # This target is active when secrets have been setup successfully.
   161	    systemd.targets.nix-bitcoin-secrets = mkIf (cfg.secretsSetupMethod != "manual") {
   162	      # This ensures that the secrets target is always activated when switching
   163	      # configurations.
   164	      # In this way `switch-to-configuration` is guaranteed to show an error
   165	      # when activating the secrets target fails on deployment.
   166	      wantedBy = [ "multi-user.target" ];
   167	    };
   168	
   169	    nix-bitcoin.setupSecrets = mkIf cfg.generateSecrets true;
   170	
   171	    nix-bitcoin.secretsSetupMethod = mkIf cfg.setupSecrets "setup-secrets";
   172	
   173	    # Operation of this service:
   174	    #  - Set owner and permissions for all used secrets
   175	    #  - Make all other secrets accessible to root only
   176	    # For all steps make sure that no secrets are copied to the nix store.
   177	    #
   178	    # The secrets dir must exist before the sandboxed service starts:
   179	    # ReadWritePaths= refuses a missing path.
   180	    systemd.tmpfiles.rules = mkIf cfg.setupSecrets [
   181	      "d '${cfg.secretsDir}' 0700 root root - -"
   182	    ];
   183	
   184	    systemd.services.setup-secrets = mkIf cfg.setupSecrets {
   185	      requiredBy = [ "nix-bitcoin-secrets.target" ];
   186	      before = [ "nix-bitcoin-secrets.target" ];
   187	      serviceConfig = {
   188	         Type = "oneshot";
   189	         RemainAfterExit = true;
   190	         # Scoped root sandbox (audit 2026-09-09, low): this is the one root
   191	         # service that generates and re-permissions every secret on the box.
   192	         # It needs to write exactly one directory and to chown/chmod files it
   193	         # does not own — nothing else. No network, no new privileges, no
   194	         # writable / outside the secrets dir.
   195	         ProtectSystem = "strict";
   196	         ReadWritePaths = [ cfg.secretsDir ];
   197	         ProtectHome = true;
   198	         PrivateTmp = true;
   199	         NoNewPrivileges = true;
   200	         RestrictAddressFamilies = [ "AF_UNIX" ];
   201	         RestrictNamespaces = true;
   202	         LockPersonality = true;
   203	         ProtectKernelTunables = true;
   204	         ProtectKernelModules = true;
   205	         ProtectControlGroups = true;
   206	         CapabilityBoundingSet = [ "CAP_CHOWN" "CAP_FOWNER" "CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE" "CAP_DAC_READ_SEARCH" ];
   207	      };
   208	      script = ''
   209	        # Use the same sort order for globbing and sorting as in Nix attrsets.
   210	        # Required for `comm` below.
   211	        export LC_COLLATE=C
   212	
   213	        ${optionalString cfg.generateSecrets ''
   214	          mkdir -p "${cfg.secretsDir}"
   215	          cd "${cfg.secretsDir}"
   216	          chown root: .
   217	          chmod 0700 .
   218	          ${cfg.generateSecretsScript}
   219	        ''}
   220	
   221	        setupSecret() {
   222	           file="$1"
   223	           user="$2"
   224	           group="$3"
   225	           permissions="$4"
   226	           if [[ ! -e $file ]]; then
   227	             echo "Error: Secret file '$file' is missing"
   228	             exit 1
   229	           fi
   230	           chown "$user:$group" "$file"
   231	           chmod "$permissions" "$file"
   232	           processedFiles+=("$file")
   233	        }
   234	
   235	        dir="${cfg.secretsDir}"
   236	        if [[ ! -e $dir ]]; then
   237	          echo "Error: Secrets dir '$dir' is missing"
   238	          exit 1
   239	        fi
   240	        chown root: "$dir"
   241	        cd "$dir"
   242	
   243	        processedFiles=()
   244	        ${
   245	          concatStrings (mapAttrsToList (n: v: ''
   246	            setupSecret ${escapeShellArg n} ${escapeShellArg v.user} ${escapeShellArg v.group} ${escapeShellArg v.permissions}
   247	          '') cfg.secrets)
   248	        }
   249	
   250	        # Make all other files accessible to root only.
   251	        # dotglob: a stray `.something` in the secrets dir must be locked down
   252	        # too, not skipped by the glob (audit 2026-08-30, low).
   253	        unprocessedFiles=$(
   254	          comm -23 <(shopt -s nullglob dotglob; printf '%s\n' * | sort) <(printf '%s\n' "''${processedFiles[@]}" | sort)
   255	        )
   256	        if [[ $unprocessedFiles ]]; then
   257	          IFS=$'\n'
   258	          # shellcheck disable=SC2086
   259	          chown root: $unprocessedFiles
   260	          # shellcheck disable=SC2086
   261	          chmod 0440 $unprocessedFiles
   262	        fi
   263	
   264	        # Now make the secrets dir accessible to other users
   265	        chmod 0751 "$dir"
   266	      '';
   267	    };
   268	  };

     1	{ lib, config, ... }:

   100	        getPublicAddressCmd = "cat ${config.nix-bitcoin.onionAddresses.dataDir}/services/${service}";
